## THE EXTRACTION CONTRACT (verbatim into every brief)
Bridge fn takes ctx: &BridgeCtx<'_> FIRST (or PARTS, below) then original params, returns
Result<T, BridgeError>. Desktop keeps every #[tauri::command] fn with an IDENTICAL signature, body:
let ctx = state.bridge_ctx(); call; .map_err(Into::into). run_*/*_with helpers a mounted test calls
STAY desktop-side as #[allow(dead_code)] AppError adapters (never pub-use over them: tests match
AppError::Core). DTOs move and return via pub use. Otherwise-unused imports kept with
#[allow(unused_imports)] // sibling *_tests.rs depends on it.
- PARITY IRON RULE: SQL, gate KIND and ORDER, lock order and COUNT, tx boundaries, event order, log text
  and fields, error variants and strings byte-identical. A non-scope-aware gate STAYS non-scope-aware.
  Never fix, tidy, dedupe, reorder or improve - REPORT. Pre-existing defects preserved on purpose.
- ctx-first has TWO sanctioned exceptions, each documented in one line: (a) a body wanting NO context
  drops ctx from BOTH sides (precedent currency_info; never write _ctx - that preserves a lie);
  (b) a caller that cannot supply a ctx passes PARTS (topology persistence's four &AppState fns;
  validate_apply_gate is the in-tree precedent). NEVER add a BridgeCtx field without an ADR.
  Filesystem paths are shim-threaded parameters - rationale at crates/oz-bridge/src/settings.rs:20-23.
  Cases: db_path (data x4 + sync_pull_scoped), app_data Option (branding), 4 x &'static str for
  env!/option_env! (health - those macros resolve PER CRATE; a verbatim move would have renamed the
  product to "oz-bridge" and blanked the target).

## THREE CLASSES OF BOUNDARY ERROR (named, each with a proven remedy)
1. TAIL POSITION 'Err(builder(..))' in return/tail/if-branch/early-return where '?' cannot apply the
   seam -> append .into(). A 'return Err(' grep MISSES the tail form (persistence.rs:644).
2. TYPE NAMES INSIDE A MOVING BODY - variant literals, hand-built struct literals, ok_or_else(|| ..)
   closures, map_err(|e| AppError::..), and '-> Result<_, AppError>' signatures - rename AS A UNIT with
   the fn. Not a 1:1 text rename: the bridge type must be named while surrounding desktop code may still
   return AppError.
3. ERROR VALUE PATTERN-MATCHED BY A ZERO-EDIT TEST -
   'matches!(r, Err(AppError::TopologyValidation { code, .. }) if code == "..")'. No visibility change,
   no '?' and no '.into()' reaches it: the mismatch is in the TEST's pattern. Remedy: a pub(crate)
   AppError-typed desktop adapter shadowing the glob (an explicitly declared item shadows a glob import).
   Cost five boxes to name. Handed to Agent-3: a desktop test matching an AppError VALUE can never run
   against bridge code without a desktop shim in the naming path.
- CFG(TEST) RULE, CAMPAIGN-WIDE (found in persistence, cost four boxes): a #[cfg(test)] item in a moved
  file MUST NOT MOVE. A dependency compiles without cfg(test), so it never exists in the bridge artifact
  and no re-export at any visibility returns it. Invisible to 'cargo check -p oz-bridge'; surfaces only as
  E0425 in the dependent crate's --tests build. Cut recipe: detect '#[cfg(test)]' not followed by
  'mod tests', walk back over contiguous '///' lines, brace-match forward, keep desktop-side VERBATIM
  (including tauri params), and REVERSE-RENAME BridgeError back to AppError before splicing.
- RE-EXPORT DEVICE: 'use' lines that look unused feed siblings or the root cfg(test) glob. Before
  dropping one, ask about the WHOLE MODULE SURFACE, not the names currently in argument (a correct
  measurement of four disputed names missed validate_topology_envelope and produced E0425). Measure on
  the POST-MOVE snapshot - a reverted tree gives the opposite verdict. But 'looks unused' is sometimes
  truly unused: cf77cbbee deleted three names proven unconsumed in BOTH builds.

G6 PIN pair: NON-recursive 'grep -rh "pub async fn" apps/desktop-client/src/commands/*.rs' = 488 AND the
   recursive count. Condition of validity: recursive 500 / delta 12 while authorize_topology_write AND
   recover_pending_topology_apply_at_startup are both desktop-side (12 = 10 IPC commands + 2 NON-commands,
   so 'delta stays 12' is the invariant and '12 commands' is not a reading).
   **AFTER (e): 499 / delta 11 - the correct post-condition per manager ruling. Do NOT keep a dead
   adapter to preserve a number; every invariant needs a stated CONDITION OF VALIDITY, not just an
   expected value.**
G7 'cargo fmt -p oz-bridge --check' and '-p oz-pos-app --check' = 0 (edition 2024; bare
   'rustfmt --edition 2021' reports falsely clean; gate at PACKAGE level - rustfmt will not fully format a
   file whose #[path] test sibling is absent).
- EVIDENCE RULES, each bought by an incident: a worktree sharing the main CARGO_TARGET_DIR can print
  'Finished' with NO 'Checking' line and no error WITHOUT compiling your bytes (proved with a deliberate
  type error); a RUNTIME binary built that way may be the other tree's image, because artifact paths are
  keyed by crate+profile, not source dir. Compile lines are provenance-bearing, runtime images are not.
  Cheapest proven runtime proof (invented by a lane): 'the same command failed in my file three minutes
  earlier and passes now'. A piped exit code belongs to the LAST pipe command, never to cargo; grep -c
  returning 0 exits 1 legitimately. 'Blocking waiting for file lock' explains an instant Finished.
  A sub-second 'Finished' means the build did not run.
- PRE-COMPILE ASSERTIONS for generated files: bridge half must contain ZERO 'use tauri', ZERO
  'crate::state', ZERO 'crate::commands', ZERO '#[tauri::command]', each import exactly once; desktop half
  exactly N '#[tauri::command]' where N = command count. Count CODE-ONLY lines (strip //, /*, *) -
  attribute greps include DOC MENTIONS (a file with 3 attributes and 5 doc references greps 5).
- COUNTING: grep the MEMBER (.db_manager, .kernel), not the receiver - rustfmt wraps chains and a
  line-anchored grep invented 38 false residuals in sync.
- missing_docs = warn fires on public STRUCT FIELDS too: skip attribute lines when judging whether a field
  is documented (a '#[serde(default)]' line produced two false NODOC reports) and re-check field
  visibility AFTER widening a struct (three warnings in revisions).

## SHARED-TREE HAZARDS PROVEN ON THIS BRANCH
- 'git commit -m subj -- <path>' takes WORKING-TREE bytes: immune to an index race, NOT to a sibling
  editing the same file mid-commit. A sibling's 'git commit --amend' ate my landed commit ab64dc906 and
  only a working-tree copy survived (restored as 2e0c46adc). A sibling 'reset HEAD~1' + 'clean -fd' or
  'stash -u' can erase uncommitted verified work with no trace.
- THEREFORE: park a copy of any new bridge file OUTSIDE the repo before declaring it; declaration and
  file arrive together or not at all. Never 'git add .', '-A', bare commit, --amend, or stash.
  Re-check 'git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> HEAD' before releasing a dependent lane.
- An unanswered hold request on a hot file = DENIAL; park the lane.
- Before any coder dispatch, read the SETTLE QUEUE, not the backlog: I once assigned one file to two lanes
  in one message.
- Foreign edits in the tree right now, NOT mine: crates/oz-core/src/db/{offline.rs,offline_tests.rs,
  quota_gate.rs} (+41/-2 - someone is editing the store layer under extractions I already certified parity
  on), ui/src/features/settings/RegionalRatesPanel.tsx, sibling journals, todo-*.md files.

   (3 commands stay desktop-side) and for local_api (whole module, 6 commands, zero shims).
2. Dialog seam for tauri_plugin_dialog (2 branding pickers) and the browser opener stay desktop-side.
3. branding set_brand_logo_path: my shim-threaded 'Option<Result<PathBuf,String>>' turned a branch that
   was unreachable in production (app: None only in cfg(test) state.rs:801-811) into an EXPLICIT PARAMETER
   - passing None silently disables the H-3 path validation. NEW RULE: prefer a non-optional type where
   the original had no None branch; where Option is genuine, document that None is a security state.
4. bridge_ctx() eager app_cache_dir() per call (a syscall + two allocations per IPC call across 450+
   shims, plus a warn path that never existed). Fix = lazy OnceLock accessor in ctx.rs + authz.rs.
5. plugins.rs placeholder deletion (needs a commands/mod.rs edit).
6. Twelve pub widenings are FORCED BY MY OWN RETAINED-ADAPTER CONTRACT (a private bridge helper cannot be
   reached from a desktop adapter), not by carelessness; they collapse when Agent-3 relocates the tests.

   the F-006 gate-balance check (while :119 uses rglob), so F-006 has NEVER examined any command under

## MY ORCHESTRATION RULES THAT BURNED (do not re-burn)
- Never journal or act on a claim I cannot quote. Verify every file:line mechanically before use: one
  dossier gave me three fictions I propagated as instructions, and five forwarded claims were wrong
  tonight, three from reliable workers. Reliability is not per-claim; label claims measured-by-me /
  by-worker / from-dossier, all requiring re-measurement.
- 'My commit landed' is a claim about HEAD at an instant.
- Sizing: command count is a poor predictor of box risk (a 32-command slice fit one box; a 9-command file
  took six). Cold shared-target compiles run 60-110s; budget for them.
- A one-shot specialist (thinker/researcher/reviewer/ops/tester/writer) CANNOT be steered mid-run; only
  spawn_coder lanes are continuable.
- A new assignment must be marked 'NEW ASSIGNMENT, BEGIN NOW' or a warm lane reads it as a status check.
- send_message / interrupt_agent need the FULL agent id; short prefixes fail.
- When offering a lane a revert option, the artifact-preservation instruction goes in the SAME message.

- ⚠️ CORRECTION TO MY OWN D-B23, AND TO AGENT-3'S D-A3.21 PIN — READ THIS BEFORE WRITING ANY TOPOLOGY RELOCATION BRIEF: 'seven test files mounted BARE at topology.rs:76-89' is WRONG. Measured: apps/desktop-client/src/commands/topology.rs is 90 lines and carries FOUR bare mounts (:77 topology_migration_guard_tests, :80 topology_projection_tests, :83 topology_stress_tests, :86 topology_validation_tests); THREE MORE live INSIDE commands/topology/commands.rs (:1058 topology_audit_tests, :1061 topology_command_tests, :1064 topology_readback_tests) — i.e. 7 blocks across 2 files, not 7 in 1. The practical consequence for a relocation pass is that the three commands.rs-mounted files must NOT be re-rooted onto the bridge topology root: the moved module already declares 'pub mod commands;' so their 'use super::commands::*;' keeps resolving, and only the mount lines move. [agent-3 hand-off]
- E9 RESCOPED FROM FOUR SUB-SLICES TO TWO. My D-B23 plan (a model+semantics -> b persistence -> c revisions -> d commands) is NOT compile-atomic: revisions.rs has 4 'use super::persistence' imports and persistence.rs has 'use super::revisions::projections_are_consistent' (a genuine cross-call, persistence.rs:312), so after (b) the crate has unresolved paths and the tree does not compile until (c) lands. The survey's verdict is a clean 2-cut at the data-layer boundary: SLICE 1 = model.rs + semantics.rs + revisions.rs + persistence.rs moved together as bridge submodules (11 'super::x' renames total — persistence 4, revisions 3, semantics 2, model 2 — and nothing else moves, so both desktop files keep their 43+29 pub items with byte-identical paths); SLICE 2 = the 10 tauri commands + the 90-line root shim body + the 10-command re-export list. A three-cut variant was rejected because it produces an intermediate commit that is neither green nor revertable. This also means E9 is TWO boxes, not four — my sizing was 2x pessimistic on this group because I sized by line count and the coupling is what costs.
- E9 SHAPE RULINGS: (1) apps/desktop-client/src/commands/topology.rs takes ZERO edits in E9 — its own 4 body moves would break 7 'use super::*' mount blocks; the desktop file's final shape is a relocation decision for Agent-3, not an extraction decision of mine. The worker flagged it, attributed it to the pre-existing non-scope-aware design documented in authz.rs, and left it byte-identical per the iron rule. Joining the ledger next to the gateway_reference-as-customer-name defect.

Wrong-ish in a way that matters for Wave-F sizing: they are not thin, they are SMALL AND UNIFORM, and the cost per command is the parity of its gate/lock/drop structure, not its line count. So 'few lines' does not mean 'cheap' — the reason this one fit a single box is that the work was generated mechanically and then gated hard, not because the commands were trivial. My earlier claim that command count predicts nothing is still true; my implied claim that small bodies mean small risk is not.

- THE REVERSAL, WITH THE EVIDENCE THAT DECIDED IT. At D-B48 I ruled that the 'db_path' need across settings/data/sync/branding justified an additive 'ctx.db_path: &'a Path' pre-wire (E0b), deferred to the Wave-E gate. The Wave-F researcher (subagent-22) came back saying that plan silently inverts a rationale recorded IN THE CODE, so I read the file instead of arguing from memory: crates/oz-bridge/src/settings.rs:20-23 says, of the hardware pair, 'BridgeCtx carries the app CACHE dir, which is a different directory from the db_path parent the shell derives, so the shim threads it in rather than having the bridge re-derive it.' And apps/desktop-client/src/commands/settings.rs:281/340/650 shows that pattern WORKING: 'let base_dir = app_data_dir(&state)?;' then the bridge call taking '&base_dir'.
- RULING: **NO ctx.db_path FIELD. EVER, UNLESS AN ADR FORCES IT.** Wave F threads paths as parameters exactly as settings does — data.rs's needs are one helper (default_backup_path at data.rs:665-666, 'state.db_path.clone()' + set_extension, called from :130 and :151) taking 'db_path: &Path'; branding's validate_logo_path (113-129, which only reaches AppHandle to get app_data_dir) takes the directory as a param, computed in the shim; sync's single sync_pull site the same. E0b is CANCELLED, and with it the entire F0 pre-wire.
- WHY THIS IS THE BETTER CALL, three reasons, in case someone re-litigates it at the gate: (1) self-consistency — I would have added a ctx field to 'solve' a problem whose solution is already shipped, documented, and working in the same crate; (2) blast radius — a new ctx field is a change to ctx.rs plus the authz.rs constructor plus the testing.rs builder, i.e. my three hottest shared files, needing a serialized window and forcing every 'BridgeCtx { .. }' literal to be updated, whereas threading a param touches two files per domain; (3) scheduling — F0 was the gate that serialized data, sync and branding behind everyone else. Remove it and all 17 Wave-F files become independent, which turns a serialized wave into a parallel one. The researcher's own warning clinched it: once db_path is in ctx, every later domain reaches for it and the parameter-threading convention dies. If a future count shows threading is hurting, the field can still be added with an ADR at that point; that door is not closing.
- WAVE F IS NOW SIZED, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN ANYBODY'S LIST SAID. Measured unextracted: 17 files, 116 commands. - THE MECHANISM, SO NEITHER OF US MISATTRIBUTES IT AGAIN: an explicit pathspec commit is immune to what the INDEX holds and blind to what a SIBLING IS WRITING — it commits the working-tree bytes of the named paths. So the repo rule that protects against sweeping staged work (never 'git add .') is NOT sufficient protection on a shared tree when two lanes are editing the SAME FILE at the same time. I knew that in the abstract, I flagged it to Agent-3 in D-B59, they did not answer, and it happened anyway. The only real control is one-owner-per-file, enforced by timing, which means: when I ask a sibling to hold a file, the ask is only valid if I treat an unanswered hold request as a DENIAL. Concretely, my briefs for the remaining settings work should have stopped until Agent-3 acknowledged; next time an unanswered cross-manager hold request on a hot file means I park that lane rather than run it.

### D-B63 (Agent-2: ⚠️ A SIBLING REWOUND THE SHARED BRANCH AND ATE MY LANDED COMMIT; pre-wire finally in with a zero-drop list; 4 lanes released, ~02:4xZ real UTC)
- * THE REWIND. `git reflog` shows 'reset: moving to HEAD~2' executed by another session inside the last hour, and it silently unlanded **ab64dc906** 'feat(bridge): add TopologyValidation to BridgeError for the topology extraction' — a commit I had VERIFIED in git log, publicly declared 'fence released' on, and built the next step against. My statement at D-B61 ('I have confirmed ab64dc906 is in git log') was true when written and false minutes later through nobody in my pool doing anything wrong. Recovery is trivial because the worker who authored it handled it perfectly: it did NOT re-commit into files outside its fence, did NOT revert them either, and left me the exact one-command fix, noting that if a sibling runs 'reset --hard' the working-tree copy dies but the object survives. Ops dispatched to restore it (pathspec commit of the two files, or cherry-pick if the tree drifted), with four merged-tree gates that must each show a real 'Checking' line, AND — the part I actually need — a full attribution table of every 'reset:' entry this hour with which dropped commits are now unreachable and whose work they were. A local reset is legal for its author and destructive to everyone else on this branch; two of them in one hour is a coordination problem, not an accident, and I want names-of-commits before I decide whether to raise it with the owner. NOTHING ELSE WAS RESTORED: I explicitly ordered ops to leave every other dropped commit alone until I have the damage list, because 'restore everything you find' on a shared tree is how you resurrect work another lane is mid-edit on.
- THE LESSON FOR MY OWN TRUST MODEL, and it is the same lesson as the target-dir false green from a different direction: **'my commit landed' is not a durable fact on this branch, it is a claim about HEAD at an instant.** I have been reasoning 'SHA is in log -> lane may proceed' all night. New rule for anything load-bearing: before releasing a dependent lane, re-check 'git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> HEAD', and when a worker tells me a fence is released, treat the released files as re-dirtyable by siblings rather than as settled. The cheaper structural fix, which I should propose to the other two managers at the gate: nobody resets a shared branch with other sessions mid-flight; squash instead by revert-commit, which is additive and cannot swallow a stranger's work.
- * PRE-WIRE LANDED, 3c4941f6b, 18 files +1609, ZERO drops: lib.rs 44 -> 59 pub mods, a new Wave-F block at :117-146 placed by the convention the worker READ FIRST (grouped by wave, alphabetical within, one /// line each) with '#[cfg(test)] mod testing;' verified still intact at :148-149 — that line is the one this campaign has dropped twice, and it asked about it unprompted. lettre added alphabetically with a comment naming the exact command that needs it, Cargo.lock +1 confined (no version churn, lettre was already in the graph at :3229 via another crate). ### D-B64 (Agent-2: * THE BRANCH REWIND WAS AN '--amend' BY A SIBLING MANAGER, IT ATE MY COMMIT AND ITS OWN FIX, restore landed 2e0c46adc; topology re-sequenced, ~02:5xZ real UTC)
- ATTRIBUTION, WITH THE EVIDENCE, AND IT IS NOT WHAT I ASSUMED. The ops pass I sent to restore one swallowed commit came back with the actual mechanism: **at 09:21:29 a sibling ran 'git commit --amend'** producing a26c5fe7e under the subject 'test(bridge): relocate audit unit tests to oz-bridge' whose 3-file +40/-12 diff ABSORBED MY 28 LINES (+15 error.rs, +13 authz.rs) UNDER THEIR TEST-COMMIT SUBJECT. So the destructive primitive on this branch is not the reset, it is THE AMEND — precisely the AGENTS.md hazard documented in the section that says an amend commits the whole index and that even verifying the index first is not sufficient, because with concurrent agents the index is a racing value. My pathspec rule has protected me from being swept; it does not protect my landed commit from someone else's amend.
- ⚠️ THE SIBLING ALSO LOST THEIR OWN WORK AND MAY NOT KNOW: **cfd126828 'fix(bridge): reorder audit test calls to bridge (ctx, token, args) signatures' is NOT in HEAD (measured 12/12 absent)**. This is not my damage to repair — their commit, their call — but it is absolutely my job to tell them, since a lane that believes a fix landed will build on it. Posted below in the hand-off.
- MY COMMIT IS RESTORED: 2e0c46adc, 2 files +28/-0, and the ops pass earned its keep by proving BEFORE committing that the working-tree bytes were 'git hash-object' == 'git rev-parse ab64dc906:<file>' identical for both files, and that HEAD matched ab64dc906^ for both, which is what made a plain pathspec commit safe rather than a cherry-pick. It also declined to 'fix' the one red gate it hit: 'cargo fmt -p oz-bridge --check' names ONLY crates/oz-bridge/src/workspaces_tests.rs, a sibling's file, so it proved its own two files clean per-file under edition 2024 and moved on. And it correctly read a trailing '[exit code: 1]' as 'grep -c warning found zero matches' rather than a build failure — the same class of misread that has burned this campaign twice.
- [AGENT-3 HAND-OFF, URGENT, EVIDENCE-BACKED]: your 'git commit --amend' at 09:21:29 (a26c5fe7e) absorbed 28 lines of MY production work (BridgeError::TopologyValidation in crates/oz-bridge/src/error.rs + the mapper arm in authz.rs) under your 'test(bridge): relocate audit unit tests' subject. - THE CONTRACT LINE I HAVE BEEN REPEATING IS SIMPLIFIED AND I AM FIXING IT EVERYWHERE: I have told ~45 slices that 'AppError -> BridgeError is a 1:1 rename'. It is not. '.ok_or(AppError::InvalidSession)' needs the bridge type named while the fn still returns AppError, and '.map_err(AppError::Core)' passes a CONSTRUCTOR that takes a CoreError and cannot be renamed at all. The survey counts 10 of those sites in the remaining two files (persistence 8, commands 2) plus 2 in revisions — and it is the idiom that actually cost my E9c lane a box, not the rarer 'return Err(topology_validation(..))' that consumed my attention. Fail-closed, therefore safe in the deny direction, but lossy for operators diagnosing why a binding is rejected. Preserved verbatim per the iron rule; it goes to the reviewer pass at the wave gate, not to a coder. Also preserved: the store-open asymmetry where 9 commands use db_manager.open_store(&session.store_id) and 7 use resolve_store (which honours restaurant_pos_id) — normalising them would be a behaviour change. And register_terminal is not in invoke_handler! at all (lib.rs:1036-1051 registers 16 of 17), a pre-existing orphan whose shim stays byte-identical anyway.
- ⚠️ [AGENT-3 HAND-OFF, BEFORE YOU RELOCATE terminals_tests.rs]: sign_binding WRITES a freshly minted uuid-v7 secret into the OS keyring when no key exists. terminals_tests.rs currently has ZERO binding/keyring references, so the existing 24 tests are keyring-safe and relocate cleanly — but any NEW bridge-side test that exercises the binding path would start writing host credentials inside 'cargo test -p oz-bridge', which is a CI-visible side effect on someone else's machine. Same class as the license wmic/reg item, arrived at from the opposite direction: there the existing tests were unsafe to move, here the existing tests are safe and only future ones are not. Also for your ledger: DEVICE_BINDING_KEYRING_NAME now exists as three literals — bridge terminals.rs (mine, pub, re-exported into the desktop), bridge workspaces.rs:34 (untouched, and its own comment says it will collapse onto oz_bridge::terminals 'when that slice lands', which it now has — that collapse is available to you or to my cleanup pass and is behaviour-visible either way), and tablet-client/commands/terminals.rs:25 (different crate, neither of our fences).

### D-B70 (Agent-2: the topology dossier is RETIRED for carrying three false load-bearing claims, Wave F at 69 of 104, ~03:4xZ real UTC)
- * A SPECIALIST DOSSIER HAS BEEN LAUNDERING FICTIONS INTO MY BRIEFS ALL HOUR, and this is the entry that says so. subagent-27's remainder survey gave me (1) 'map_err(AppError::Core) at commands.rs:571 and :641' — the lines are an open_store call and a closing brace; (2) 'ok_or(AppError::InvalidSession) x10 across the two remaining files' — the true count of any ok_or-shaped site is THREE, all ok_or_else, and the InvalidSession form occurs ZERO times in the whole group; (3) 'authorize_topology_write is called from SEVEN sites, four inside persistence.rs', which is the claim that made me rule that persistence and commands must move in one 2,050-line box to avoid a nested third-guard deadlock. E9c measured the truth: TWO call sites, both inside commands.rs (:88, :131), zero in persistence.rs, and persistence names no function defined in commands.rs, so it is a strict lower layer that moves alone and green. There is no adapter, therefore no third acquisition, therefore no coupling. All three numbers were wrong, all three reached workers as my instructions, and the third one changed the shape of the campaign's last box.
- SCOPE THE INVALIDATION HONESTLY RATHER THAN TRASHING THE WORKER: its claims about OTHER domains have held up under independent verification every single time — tables' zero-gap rating (confirmed by the tables lane: resolve_store x3, six gated writes, drop(db) preserved), health's env::var and UdpSocket census, offline's zero-gap and mid-file mount, history/email/local_payment's gap claims and the three pg_sync blocked sites, terminals' keyring finding. What failed is its topology error-and-lock census, the one part I never asked a worker to re-derive before acting on it. So the dossier is retired for that section only, and the rule it teaches is general: **a specialist's number becomes my instruction only after somebody who edits the file has re-measured it.** The 'combine the two files' recommendation goes with its evidence.
- MY OWN ERROR MULTIPLIED BECAUSE I PASSED IT ON TWICE: I first wrote the fictional :571/:641 sites into E9c's brief, then when E9c told me they did not exist I quoted its correction onward into E9d's brief as 'measured by worker' — but I had also re-asserted the 'seven authorize sites' claim in the SAME message, in bold, as the reason for a ruling. The lesson is not 'distrust dossiers', it is that a claim only earns trust by being tested, and I tested one of two contradicting reports and not the other. E9d went out with the split reversed, my earlier 'do not propose splitting them again' explicitly withdrawn, and the worker's own recommendation adopted on its evidence.
- THE &AppState RULING, WHICH IS THE FIRST SIGNATURE-SHAPE EXCEPTION THE CAMPAIGN HAS HAD TO MAKE: recover_pending_topology_apply_at_startup is 'pub async fn (&AppState)' at persistence.rs:448 and its caller is lib.rs:135 through the zero-edit root re-export, and bridge_ctx() exists only on State<'_,AppState> — the tauri type — so from a bare &AppState there is no ctx to hand a moved function. Two ways out and I took the second: NOT an FCR to change lib.rs (the most contested file in the campaign) and NOT a new BridgeCtx::from_app_state constructor, which would have bought signature uniformity at the price of a 16-field builder in desktop code that every later lane could drift against. Instead the three helpers take the PARTS they actually use (connection handle, StoreDatabaseManager, apply-lock, all three already on AppState, and the apply-lock is already a ctx field so the shape is precedented), with the desktop &AppState adapter reaching them off its own argument. They therefore break 'ctx first' for the only defensible reason a caller can have, each with a one-line doc-comment justification, because the next reader will otherwise 'fix' it. This extends the currency_info precedent, which E9c found in-tree and I have now made a rule: for a body that wants no context, drop it from both sides; never invent a use; never write '_ctx', because that preserves a lie about the interface.
- WAVE F IS AT 69 OF 104 with three fresh commits from the lanes I released after the wiring landed: 077c039c9 tables (bridge 5->199, desktop 213->141, 9 commands), 27f5122da health (8 commands), and the 17 between them ran 20/20 desktop tests through the shims. Health contributed a genuinely new hazard class: env! and option_env! resolve PER-CRATE at compile time, so a verbatim move of version/version_scoped would have silently changed the wire — 'name' from 'oz-pos-app' to 'oz-bridge' and 'target' to 'unknown'. The lane threaded the four macro reads as &'static str parameters from the shim instead, kept the runtime probes verbatim as ordered, and made the same ctx-drop call for ping/get_device_id/get_local_ip. That is now in the contract text for every remaining lane, because branding and browser have the same shape.
- THREE LANES ARE NOW IDLE AFTER LANDING, so the pool is at six again: 3711a033 on (d) persistence alone, then (e) commands plus authorize carrying the trigger subject, which it is forbidden to spend early; 308b5a6c on BRANDING, eight of ten commands with the two tauri_plugin_dialog pickers staying desktop-side byte-identical per the open_in_browser precedent, and an explicit order to measure the asset-write paths before porting and thread them rather than reach for a ctx field; 6d2e47fe on BROWSER (URL builder and its allow-list move, the platform opener stays) then FEATURES, where I warned it that 4 commands in 647 lines means the other 600 are helpers and DTOs that mounted tests may call. cc94fe8f, 216a4421 and d9f1e34b stay on their in-flight sweeps, offline and sync.

- OFFLINE LANDED bd9cbf3c8 — bridge 5->441, desktop 435->155, 9 commands, 8/8 of the domain's tests executing through the shims, mid-file mount preserved at :44 in its original relationship (adapters above, scoped commands below), three private helpers retained desktop-side as AppError adapters because the mount calls them directly and one matches on AppError::Core { sub_kind }, six DTOs moved and returned by pub use, four SYNC_MANAGE gates still after resolve_scope, one global lock still inside its original inner scope. Wave F is now 78 of 104.
- THE BEST ENGINEERING LESSON OF THE WAVE, from its postmortem, and it goes into every generating lane's brief: my worker's static checker compared command and helper BODIES and came out at zero mismatches, while the actual defect was in a region it never looked at — its block splitter flushed the desktop file's whole original import block into the bridge header, producing eleven E0252 duplicates plus 'use tauri::State', 'crate::state::AppState' and 'crate::commands::authz' in a headless crate, and separately kept nine '#[tauri::command]' attributes it had only stripped on the desktop side. **A check that passes on the wrong region is worse than no check, because it looks like evidence.** I had warned it that a glob collision was the likely mechanism; the real mechanism was adjacent and my diagnosis was close enough to be useless, which is the same failure as the dossier numbers in a milder form — I reasoned about the shape of the defect instead of asking for the file.

Cheap, mechanical, and it would have caught the whole 15-error red window before the rest of the pool saw it.

Two lanes on one file. Pathspec commits do not protect against this: they take working-tree bytes, so two owners of one file is a real collision no matter how disciplined the git hygiene. I caught it before either wrote (the file is still the 6-line stub) and reassigned browser-only to 6d2e47fe, explicitly ordering it to say so if it had already opened the file. ### D-B72 (Agent-2: (d) is fully designed by its own worker, my third wrong list in this group, and a grep lesson that applies campaign-wide, ~03:4xZ real UTC)
- E9d came back Blocked-on-time with ZERO edits and a complete verified transform, which is the right outcome: it refused to start a 990-line port with ~8 minutes and cold 60-70s compiles, because the instruction that siblings must never inherit an uncompilable lib outranks its own desire to attempt it. It also explicitly declined to claim a green it had not observed since HEAD moved — the discipline I have been buying all night is now standard.
- MY LIST OF THE &AppState FUNCTIONS WAS WRONG at its third member for the third time in this group. Reality, measured: four of them — :448 recover_pending_topology_apply_at_startup (needs db + apply_lock), :467 recover_pending_topology_apply (db), :519 snapshot_workspace_rows (db_manager), :559 compensate_workspace_diff (db_manager) — three distinct parts across four fns, none needing more than two, and all three parts already exist as ctx fields so naming them as parameters introduces no new shared surface. validate_apply_gate at :664 is NOT an AppState fn at all: it already takes '&[&Connection]' plus values, and topology_tests.rs:790/:903 call it with hand-built slices, which makes it the in-tree PRECEDENT for the parts-passing ruling rather than a case needing one.
- ⚠️ THE GREP LESSON, AND IT CAME FROM A WORKER CORRECTING ITSELF: last box it told me the dossier was wrong that persistence uses state.db_manager. This box it found the call is written across three lines — ':524-526 let conn = state / .db_manager / .open_store(store_id)' — so its own '^state\.'-style pattern had silently missed it, and the dossier was right after all. **A line-anchored grep undercounts any method chain that rustfmt is allowed to wrap**, and this codebase is edition-2024 formatted with long chains wrapping routinely. It is the same class as the tail-position 'Err(...)' with no 'return' that my seven-site count missed, and the same class as the header region a body-only diff cannot see: three different tools, one failure mode — **the pattern matches what it was shaped to match, not what is there.** New brief text: grep the MEMBER name (.db_manager, .kernel, topology_validation) not the receiver, and when a count is load-bearing, get it two ways.
- (i) OVER (ii), ON THE REASON I ALWAYS WANT ON THE RECORD: per-fn parts, minimum surface, matching the existing validate_apply_gate idiom. The rejected alternative would have reshaped two store-opening call paths to buy a symmetrical signature, and 'it looks more consistent' has never once justified touching a second code path in a parity-neutral refactor.
- DOCS TAIL PRE-AUTHORISED WITH A CIRCUIT BREAKER ATTACHED, because this is the one place where the two standing rules genuinely pull against each other: ~50 pub(crate)->pub widenings are needed for the root's cfg(test) glob to keep resolving, and 'missing_docs = warn' plus my zero-warning gate means each newly public item needs a real '///'. Writing those docs is NOT a parity violation (no behaviour, no string, no SQL changes), so lanes may do it — but they may NOT solve it with '#[allow(missing_docs)]' on the module, or '#[doc(hidden)]', or by narrowing a fn back to pub(crate) and breaking the glob, all three of which would trade a visible warning for an invisible lie about the API. And the honest stop-condition: if the undocumented tail is 15+ fns, report the count instead of typing fifty doc comments, because at that size the correct conclusion is that the tests belong in the bridge and the widening should not happen at all. Ten is a task; thirty is a design signal, and a green gate that hides the signal is worth less than a red one that shows it.
- SHADOWING FACT WORTH KEEPING: an explicitly declared item shadows a glob-imported one, so the four desktop adapters can coexist with 'pub use oz_bridge::topology::persistence::*;' with no ambiguity error — which is what makes (d) landable without touching commands.rs, and by its analysis (e) needs no commands.rs change either beyond its own move. topology_command_tests.rs calls recover_pending_topology_apply(&state, store_id) at FOUR sites (:1135, :1187, :1245, :2034) and lib.rs:135 calls the at-startup variant, so both names must keep answering with &AppState and AppError desktop-side, permanently, until Agent-3 relocates that test file.

### D-B77 (Agent-2: * I dropped a whole extraction step and it put the shared bridge crate in a red state, browser landed, ~04:0xZ real UTC)
- THE ERROR, IN PLAIN TERMS. The topology dependency chain is LINEAR: model ← semantics ← **revisions** ← persistence ← commands. When I accepted E9c's (correct) finding that persistence and commands need not be combined, I collapsed the plan from four steps to two and **deleted revisions from the sequence**. I then ruled 'persistence moves alone and green'. It cannot: persistence's own device lines at :16-18 are 'use super::model:: / super::revisions:: / super::semantics::', and in the bridge there is no revisions module, so the move produces error[E0432] unresolved import super::revisions. The lane has an UNTRACKED crates/oz-bridge/src/topology/persistence.rs plus a ' M' topology.rs that DECLARES it — which is the worst available state, because the lib does not build for anyone and none of its work is committed. Four coders and two reviewers were blocked through it at the moment I found out.
- HOW IT REACHED ME, AND IT IS WORTH NOTING: not from my own check, and not from the lane, but from the browser worker citing a foreign red it refused to chase. That is the third time today a lane's discipline in REPORTING an unfounded-looking red is what surfaced one of my errors — the settings orphan warning, the data.rs unused ctx, and now this. My briefs ask for exactly that and it has paid for itself three times over.
- WHAT I GOT RIGHT IN THE SAME BREATH, SO THE DIAGNOSIS IS NOT 'I was careful enough': E9c's report had told me persistence's device lines 'must survive the move' and I filed that as a detail to preserve rather than reading it as the dependency it was. The information was in front of me. A device line IS an edge: if module A says 'use super::B::*' and that line must survive, then B must exist on the far side of the boundary before A can cross it. **New rule for every group with an internal chain: derive the move order from the surviving import lines, not from the file sizes, and after any re-slicing re-derive it once more — because the step I dropped was not hidden in the data, it was hidden in my summary of the data.**
- IMMEDIATE ACTION TAKEN, within one message of learning it: urgent steer to 3711a033 with the root cause named as mine, two options keyed to its clock — (i) revert the declaration and the untracked file, port REVISIONS as its own commit, keep persistence for the next box; or (ii) revert, prove the lib green, and hold while I dispatch revisions elsewhere — with the FIRST instruction being 'make the lib compilable again' in either branch. Untracked-plus-declared is the one state that has no recovery story if the lane dies mid-box.
- BROWSER LANDED 84ec9593f — bridge 5->85, desktop 91->51, one command, residual 0 across three units, 2/2 runtime tests executing the bridge's encoder through the pub use, hook run fully with no --no-verify, and the pin pair reported the new way (488 top-level / 500 recursive, delta 12 explained by name). ### D-B78 (Agent-2: features in, and a lane just invalidated its own runtime evidence, which is the most important thing I have learned about my gate design tonight, ~04:2xZ real UTC)
- FEATURES LANDED b435f8be6 (bridge 6->640, desktop 647->119, 4 commands, 23/23 of the domain's tests, the ~240-line metadata table moved as one byte-exact block, device_hostname verbatim with no seam, terminal_id read through the FIELD with the reason in the commit body as ruled). Wave F extraction is now COMPLETE except the parked stays — three pg_sync commands, two dialog pickers, the browser opener, local_api and the plugins placeholder.
- * THE EVIDENCE DOCTRINE NEEDED A THIRD REVISION AND THE LANE DID IT TO ITSELF: features reported G4 as '23 passed; 0 failed' and then attached the caveat that the executed image was '…oz-pos-app_lib-a5ebd0b88611bf70.exe', a hash EQUAL TO THE MAIN TREE'S, so with a single shared CARGO_TARGET_DIR **it cannot prove the binary it ran contained its bytes** even though the worktree 'Compiling' line preceded the run. I have spent the last two hours treating the merged-tree runtime test as the strongest evidence and treating a worktree COMPILE with a visible 'Checking <path>' line as adequate substitute evidence. That second half is now only half true: **compile lines are provenance-bearing, runtime binaries are not**, because the artefact path is keyed by crate and profile, not by source directory. Consequence, applied from this box onward: runtime proof must come from the merged tree or from a worktree with a PRIVATE --target-dir, and where a lane cannot get that, it says so and leans on its static assertion set instead, exactly as this lane did. I would rather have a report that degrades its own evidence honestly than one that inflates it, and I have now told the topology lane this in writing.
- THE PRE-COMPILE ASSERTIONS CAUGHT A REAL BUG BEFORE THE COMPILER DID, which is the point of them: the features generator was REJECTED by its own gate on the first pass — 5 desktop '#[tauri::command]' lines where 4 were expected (one was in my own '//!' prose) and 3 'state' tokens in the bridge half (all doc words like 'current state and metadata'). Its fix was to tighten the assertion to CODE-ONLY lines by stripping comments before matching, and it framed that as applying my own 'a pattern sees only what it was shaped to see' rule to its own checker. That is the second time a lane has taken a lesson I wrote from someone else's failure and used it on their own tooling, and it is the first time the class of bug — a duplicated desktop import block leaking into a headless file — got caught at generation instead of at compile.
- FIVE MORE FINDINGS, PRESERVED NOT FIXED, and one of them is a doc claim I have been repeating: features.rs says 'all 32 features' while the metadata table and the test enumeration both carry **39 Feature:: variants** — documentation drift in the direction that understates the surface. Also: 'device_hostname' is itself UNDOCUMENTED because its doc block is fused into the run above feature_to_module_id's; the 8-line audit stamp's closing '*/' shares a line with the first '//!' (copied verbatim, and the lane's generator handles everything before the first 'use' as a head, which is what caught the offline leak class); and 'list_all_features' reads the GLOBAL db while 'list_all_features_scoped' reads the scope's STORE db — the same asymmetry flagged for terminals and branding, now three modules deep, which stops being three coincidences the next time I look at it. Two desktop helpers are now tests-only adapters, joining the cleanup list.
- MY SEQUENCING ERROR IS NOW FULLY CORRECTED BY THE LANE THAT TRIPPED OVER IT: (d) cannot precede revisions, and the reason is two names in one device line — persistence.rs:273 puts 'Option<&TopologyRevisionContext<'_>>' on a PUBLIC signature and :336 calls 'insert_topology_revision(…)' inside the save transaction. Both of my earlier claims were true and beside the point: 'persistence reaches no function defined in commands.rs' (irrelevant, the edge goes upward into revisions) and 'revisions is a strict lower layer' (never verified in the direction that mattered). Revisions is now dispatched as its own green commit — ~430 lines, zero command attributes, zero AppState reach-ins — then persistence as the second, then commands with the trigger subject.
- ONE SMALL ACT OF SELF-PRESERVATION I AM RATIFYING RATHER THAN CLEANING: the lane left its 995-line port on disk as an UNTRACKED file — undeclared, therefore inert to both cargo and rustfmt — so the next box re-lands it by adding one mod line and an 88-line shim instead of re-deriving 995 verified lines. I approved keeping it AND ordered a copy parked OUTSIDE the repo, because this branch has already demonstrated the hazard: a sibling's 'git commit --amend' ate my landed commit earlier tonight and only a working-tree copy saved it, which means a sibling 'git clean -fd' or 'git stash -u' would erase a day of verified work with no trace in any message. Tidiness is not worth that exposure, and 'one ?? entry' is the cheapest insurance in the campaign.

I am NOT refilling the idle coder slots, and the reason is a collision, not an absence of work: everything left in the extraction backlog is in the topology chain, which one lane must walk in dependency order because the failure mode I just created was two lanes reading different halves of one import graph. ### D-B80 (Agent-2: the measurement pass came back with a shared-gate blind spot, a clean file-size verdict, and the real shape of what is left, ~04:2xZ real UTC)
- Q1, THE ANSWER I ASKED FOR WAS A PROXY FOR SOMETHING BIGGER. The F-006 'unregistered command fns' counter is a **FLOOR, NOT A COUNT**: the script's regex at scripts/verify-ipc-parity.py:66-68 requires '#[tauri::command]' to be IMMEDIATELY followed by 'pub [async] fn', so any command with a doc comment between the attribute and the signature is invisible to it. Measured with a tolerant parser: **41 unregistered '#[tauri::command]' fns, where the script reports 16** — 25 hidden, clustered in reports.rs (7), customers.rs (5), settings.rs (3), hardware.rs (3), currencies.rs (2), staff.rs (2), exchange_rates.rs (2). The counter is informational and never fails the gate, so this is not a broken build, it is a broken MEASUREMENT in a shared script that both sibling managers and I have been reading as a surface census. [Fact: subagent-30, measured twice + across 10 revisions]
- AND MY LANES CAUSED THE DRIFT I ASKED ABOUT, which is the part I have to own: 'update_sync_settings' became counted during Wave F not because it newly lost a registration — lib.rs registers only the '_scoped' form and the unscoped one was dropped back at e8e8f4dd6 'security(H-1/H-2): unregister 77 deprecated unscoped commands' — but because the shim generator **placed the doc comment ABOVE the attribute** where the original had it BELOW. So a cosmetic ordering choice in my own extraction flipped a shared gate's counter. Two consequences: (1) my standing claim that shims keep their headers 'byte-identical' has a systematic exception in line ORDER (content preserved, order not), and (2) do NOT churn 90 shims to restore the order — that is a wave of pure diff for no behaviour change; instead the rule goes forward only, and the ADR records the counter's real semantics.
- Q2, A CLEAN VERDICT ON THE 1,000-LINE RULE, and it is better than I feared: six production files are over 1,000 today (bridge pos.rs 1,799, staff.rs 1,401, auth.rs 1,344, settings.rs 1,179, desktop topology/commands.rs 1,060, bridge products.rs 1,029). Baseline at de293ff66^ (parent of the campaign scaffold, measured per file) shows **five of the six were already over the cap before any of us started**, in desktop form — pos 1,793, staff 1,332, auth 1,294, settings 1,215, topology/commands 1,060 untouched. **Exactly ONE breach was created by this campaign: crates/oz-bridge/src/products.rs at 1,029, whose desktop source was 939** — an Agent-1 split that crossed the line rather than staying under it. Net posture: 5 desktop breaches at baseline, now 1 desktop + 5 bridge, so the extraction has been broadly size-neutral and has moved the problem rather than growing it. Desktop side is now empty in the 700-1,000 band; eight of the nine files in that band are bridge modules.
- Q3, THE MAP I SHOULD HAVE HAD BEFORE SLICING WAVE F: under a strict reading, the desktop command layer is **450 shims / 3 stubs / 37 inline**, with 844 inline code-lines, and **47 of 58 files are already 100% shimmed**. The branding logo-path branch that skips H-3 validation used to be gated by PROCESS STATE: 'app: None' exists only in '#[cfg(test)] AppState::for_test' (state.rs:801-811), so it was unreachable in production and unreachable over IPC. My shim-threaded-parameter pattern turned it into an **Option argument a caller opts into** — so as the reviewer puts it, any future shim that passes None silently disables the validation, and the escape hatch is now explicit rather than incidental. It is still parity-neutral and it is still the only shape that keeps the branch's behaviour byte-identical, so I am not reverting it; the follow-up (make the unvalidated branch a compile-time requirement instead of an optional argument) is a designed change to a security control and belongs to its owner lane, not to me mid-extraction. What I have changed is the RULE for every future threaded parameter: prefer a NON-optional type where the original had no None branch, and where Option genuinely exists because the original had one, name the failure mode in the parameter's doc comment so the next shim author knows that None is a security state, not a convenience.

Identical at 168e7063b~1, byte-for-byte, so the extraction is not implicated; but a support engineer chasing 'tampering detected' on a healthy terminal now has a documented mechanism to check first. ### D-B88 (Agent-2: independent parity review returns APPROVED with one systemic Minor that is bigger than its rating, ~05:5xZ real UTC)
- VERDICT: Approved on terminals/offline/sync, 0 Critical, 0 Major, 1 Minor, 3 Nit. No parity break in any command body, gate, lock, error or event across 42 bodies read in full. And it got there with a method designed to be immune to the two blind spots I have been handing out all night: brace-matched per-function extraction with wrapped chains JOINED so a rustfmt reflow cannot fake a mismatch, plus an ORDER-INDEPENDENT WHOLE-FILE LINE MULTISET diff that catches anything outside fn bodies (derives, serde attributes, imports, moved statements), plus a separate import/residue diff across all six files. That third one is what would have found the duplicated desktop import block at generation time.
- THE PART I WOULD NEVER HAVE FOUND BY SELF-REPORTING, because it is not in any command body: **'[Minor] shims add an unrequested syscall and a possible new log line to all 39 commands.'** bridge_ctx() at apps/desktop-client/src/commands/authz.rs:186-201 resolves app.path().app_cache_dir() ON EVERY CALL and on failure emits 'tracing::warn!(resolving app cache dir: {e})', plus an AppHandle::clone() and an Arc::new(TauriEventSink) at :215-220. The original bodies never touched it — list_terminals_scoped at 168e7063b^ went straight to resolve_session. And none of the three modules reads that field: media_cache_dir, emitter, registry, plugins, scanner_cancel, topology_apply_lock and session_ttl score ZERO hits across all three. So this is (a) one filesystem-path resolution plus two allocations per IPC call on 39 commands so far, which scales to every extracted command in the campaign — over 450 by the researcher's census, and (b) a NEW log line that can appear on paths where no log line existed, which the parity iron rule does not license even though the reviewer's own confidence is that it is behaviourally inert. It graded this Minor because it is inert; I am escalating the FIX priority because it is systemic and because 'a warn that did not exist before' is the kind of thing an operator sees and a test that asserts on log output eventually trips over.
- DECISION ON IT: not this minute, and the reason is a fence not an opinion. The fix is to make the cache-dir resolution lazy — a OnceLock behind an accessor rather than an eager field in BridgeCtx — which means editing crates/oz-bridge/src/ctx.rs AND the desktop shim that builds it, while the persistence lane is mid-box with 990 lines depending on the current ctx shape. It goes at the head of the post-Wave-E queue with the reviewer's citations attached, and until then the campaign's honest statement is that every shim pays an app_cache_dir() resolution per call. Recorded rather than glossed.
- THE CREDIBILITY NIT, ANSWERED, AND THE NIT WAS RIGHT TO BE RAISED: for terminals the extraction commit 168e7063b touched ONE FILE, the desktop half, because the 822-line bridge body had already landed inside the Wave-F pre-wire 3c4941f6b at my order — so any 'substitution-residual count' computed from that commit's own diff structurally could not have seen the ported body. I checked the two reports rather than assume: the terminals lane diffed '168e7063b^:desktop' against the COMMITTED BRIDGE FILE at current HEAD, naming '3c4941f6b, not the tree I parked on', and the memo lane likewise took its originals from '3c4941f6b:apps/desktop-client/src/commands/*.rs' and compared against the bridge bodies. Box four produced the actual rule. What IS mine is that my two rulings were both about VISIBILITY, and visibility was never the variable.

Check before you generate: 'grep -c "cfg(test)" <file>' and read every hit that is not the mount. ### D-B93 (Agent-2: my own journal carried an UNVERIFIED CLOCK for ~12 entries, including the block that survives compaction. VERIFIED UTC: 2026-09-11T11:23:34Z from date -u)
- FACT: date -u prints 11:23:34Z. My recent entries (D-B81 onward, plus the header of the new current-state ledger) say roughly 06:0xZ to 06:4xZ. Either those were written at times I had genuinely re-read and more than five hours have passed, or I wrote them from memory of a clock reading taken long before. I cannot now tell which, and that ambiguity IS the finding: an unverifiable timestamp is not evidence, no matter how precise it looks.
- MECHANISM: my standing rule is that timestamps come from date -u, never from memory. I wrote that rule into this journal after an earlier slip and broke it again in the same session, in the same file, inside the block whose entire job is to survive context loss. The reason is mundane and worth naming: I re-read the clock whenever I run a shell command anyway, and between shell commands I compose entries from the last number I saw. Writing journal entries is precisely the activity that has no shell step in it.
- CONSEQUENCE IF LEFT: the ledger is the artifact a future session trusts most, because it is the only part guaranteed to be in context. A five-hour clock error inside it does not look like an error, it looks like chronology. I have spent this campaign ordering sibling hand-offs, attributing which session clobbered which commit, and settling claims of the form my-entry-predates-yours by timestamp; every such judgement in the affected window is now weakened in one specific way: order WITHIN a run of entries I wrote between two clock reads is fine (they were written in sequence), but order ACROSS sessions is not re-derivable from what I recorded.
- MITIGATION, AND IT IS THE GENERAL ONE: git is the clock. Every claim in this journal that matters also has a commit, and git log with committer timestamps gives real, dated evidence for my lanes and both siblings. So forward: (1) anything whose ordering against another session matters cites a SHA, never a wall-clock time alone; (2) I re-read date -u in the SAME bash call that appends the entry, one command, no memory in the loop - the fix that actually holds because it removes the step where remembering is required; (3) the ledger header now carries the verified anchor, so a future reader can date-correct the window instead of trusting it.
- ALSO TRUE, AND IT CUTS AGAINST ME NOT FOR THEM: the error does not flatter anyone. It makes no count look better and no lane look faster, and every number that decides anything tonight - 365 passed, 727 passed, 452/448/27, pin 488/500 - is re-measurable from the tree and was re-measured. A clock is the cheapest thing to verify and the easiest to fake from memory, which is exactly why it is where I should look first when I want to catch myself.
- LEDGER MECHANICS COMPLETED IN THIS WINDOW, since they were the point of the pass: condensed current-state ledger (17 KB, ASCII) PREPENDED to manager-2-journal.md as the block that survives the ~131 KB injection cut; file repaired from 75 CESU-8 bytes that had made it unreadable to the read tool (emoji through bash heredocs - a transport defect, not an intent, so journal writes are ASCII from here); pre-repair backup parked at C:/Users/Dika/AppData/Local/Temp/m2j-backup.md; sibling sections untouched, 85 D-B headers present before and after the patch, strict UTF-8 validation now passing on the whole file.

    questions. Same overrun, opposite decision, and the decision rule is written down so the next one is

  confirms my ruling and exposes a flaw in my own grep): at HEAD, 'authorize_topology_write' appears in
  desktop commands/topology/commands.rs AND in commands/topology/topology_command_tests.rs (a test file
  that STILL EXISTS and is still mounted). Superficially that refutes 'no test roots it'. It does not:
  my pattern was '[^a-zA-Z_]NAME[^(]' - a MENTION detector that deliberately EXCLUDES call syntax, so a
  file hit says nothing about callers. The authority is the compiler, which I already have: at 12:17Z the
  SAME test file was mounted and compiling under 'cargo check -p oz-pos-app --tests', and the adapter
  still emitted a dead_code warning. An item with a live caller in the --tests build cannot warn as dead.
  So #[allow(dead_code)] over #[cfg(test)] stands - but note the meta-lesson: I built a regex that could
  only produce a mention count and then nearly read a caller verdict out of it. Every measurement must
  name what it can and CANNOT prove.

  including apps/desktop-client/src/lib.rs (the retained at-startup adapter path) and the still-present

TWO NEW EVIDENCE RULES, each bought this pass:
  (1) cargo REPLAYS CACHED WARNINGS. A warning line is therefore NOT proof of compilation; only a
      'Checking'/'Compiling' line naming the crate is. My rules covered 'Finished with no errors' but not
      a warning echoed from cache - which also means a stale warning can look like a fresh regression.
  (2) tools.bash declares NO run_in_background and SILENTLY IGNORES the flag: my 5-step gate script ran in
      the foreground, died at the 300s ceiling mid-G4, and reset the persistent shell. tools.pwsh DOES
      support backgrounding - it is the only route for a cargo sequence longer than ~4 minutes.

topology chain), per my own wave-gate rule that a red gate earns a small mechanical fix dispatch rather
than another patch-by-vibes. Scope, one commit, explicit pathspec: 2 dead-code annotations (with the
cfg(test) trap restated - never write an annotation that preserves a lie), the 2 malformed header joins,

equals HEAD for its 3 files after). Files: apps/desktop-client/src/commands/topology/persistence.rs,

6.24s, exit 0, 0 warnings - the RED is gone with provenance, not by cache echo), plus both package
fmt checks. It honestly flagged what it did NOT re-run (G5 parity, G6 pin) because its change touches
no command surface; I am re-running those anyway at the consolidated gate rather than accepting the
argument - an argument about what a change cannot affect is exactly the kind of claim I have been
burned by all night.

**NEW HAZARD CLASS, discovered by the lane, cost nothing to learn and is now in the contract:**
converting a '#[cfg(test)]' item to '#[allow(dead_code)]' can break the PLAIN (non-test) build with
E0425 when the wrapper was the ONLY thing naming an also-cfg(test)-gated callee. Observed on
save_topology_json -> save_topology_json_at_key: un-gating the wrapper makes the plain build compile
its body, which then references a callee that only exists under cfg(test). So the wrapper was not
merely unused, it was load-bearing for the CALLEE'S visibility. Corollary: a cfg(test) item can be
simultaneously (a) unreachable by any remaining test and (b) structurally depended on - which is why
'annotate it' and 'delete it' are NOT interchangeable remedies here. The lane's decision to DELETE
save_topology_json (doc literally said 'Do NOT wire it into production') and ANNOTATE
validate_semantic_ownership is accepted: both-build proof was run before each choice, and G3 shows no
new warning, so save_topology_json_at_key stayed rooted.

Its doc fixes landed too: the correctness reviewer's malformed header joins were split at
semantics.rs:7 and persistence.rs:**5** (my brief said :6 - it located the glue by content, which is
the right instinct; my line number was an off-by-one I should not have passed on as fact), and the
three pub-reachability precondition lines are in: recover_pending_topology_apply (caller holds apply
lock + command-layer gate; no internal gate), snapshot_workspace_rows and compensate_workspace_diff
(internally ungated, authorized-Apply-window only, self-lock the store conn). Comment/annotation-only
diff - no behaviour, SQL, gate order, lock count, or error text touched, verified against the stat.

### D-A3.94 (Phase 3.3 T5 auth — BUG #4, a SECURITY one: the tablet silently dropped the picker ticket and minted sessions unverified. Committed 677ec13f4c)
- HOW IT SURFACED: the T5 audit diffed tablet DTOs against bridge twins and flagged exactly one auth divergence — CreateSessionArgs, tablet 7 fields vs bridge 8 (b-only: picker_ticket). Confirmed against the UI: ui/src/api/staff.ts::CreateSessionArgs declares picker_ticket MANDATORY ("HMAC-signed picker ticket from staff_login / bootstrap_owner"), and the UI's snake_case wire (user_id/role_id/.../picker_ticket/org_id) matches the tablet DTO's no-rename shape exactly — so the field deserialized... into nothing. No deny_unknown_fields on that DTO, so serde dropped it silently. Same class as the attemptId incident, on the auth path.
- SEVERITY (verified both sides, not inferred): the BRIDGE create_session runs H-3 — `picker::verify_picker_ticket(&ctx.picker_ticket_secret, &args.picker_ticket, now_ts)` and then requires `verified_user_id == args.user_id` ("Invalid or expired picker ticket", uniform denial, tracing::warn both branches). The TABLET create_session had NO ticket step at all (grepped its body: only empty-field validation → instance access → subscription → mint). So the tablet minted a session for any caller-supplied user_id/role_id: verify_instance_access proves that USER holds a role for that instance, never that the CALLER is that user. A staff-role holder could mint an owner session on the tablet by naming user-owner. Desktop was never affected (same bridge path as always).
- FIX (2 files, +145/−126): apps/tablet-client/src/commands/auth.rs — (a) `pub picker_ticket: String` restored on CreateSessionArgs, doc'd with why it vanished; (b) the H-3 gate inserted verbatim from the bridge after the empty-field check and BEFORE any DB/session work: verify against `state.picker_ticket_secret` (the same secret staff_login already signs with — the machinery was all present, only the gate was missing), derive user_id from the ticket, refuse a mismatch with the identical uniform message. auth_tests.rs — a `test_ticket()` helper signing under the for_test secret (`b"test-picker-ticket-secret"`), 15 existing CreateSessionArgs literals updated so they pass REAL tickets through the restored gate (so forged-role/unknown-user tests still fail for their ORIGINAL reason, not the ticket gate), + 2 new pins: create_session_rejects_forged_picker_ticket (valid signature bound to the WRONG user must be refused AND leave session_store empty; garbage ticket denied the same uniform way) and create_session_args_require_the_ui_wire_picker_ticket (accepts the exact snake_case UI payload; a payload without the ticket must fail loudly naming the field).
- ALSO IN THIS SLICE: the other 9 auth DTOs re-exported from oz_bridge::auth (audit confirmed all field-identical + same casing — pure dedup); unused serde import swept. Bodies stay tablet-native.
- GATES: tablet check CLEAN 0 warnings; tablet --lib 652/652 (auth 37/37 — was 35, +2 new); fmt both crates; committed with wtree-guard own→verify.
- RULING: a shell-local DTO that is a strict SUBSET of its bridge twin is not "a smaller surface", it is a dropped field until proven otherwise. The audit that found this costs one diff; the bug it found was a live auth bypass on shipped hardware.
